2. *name* shall be `None` or a `str` ("arg 3 (name) must be None or string"),
   defaulting to *code*.`co_name`; *argdefs* `None` or a tuple ("arg 4
   (defaults) must be None or tuple"); *kwdefaults* `None` or a dict.
3. *closure* shall be `None` or a tuple ("arg 5 (closure) must be tuple") of
   cells ("arg 5 (closure) expected cell, found *tname*") whose length equals
   the number of free variables of *code* ("*name* requires closure of length
   *n*, not *m*").
4. Return a new function whose `__qualname__` is *code*.`co_qualname`,
   `__module__` is *globals*`["__name__"]` if present, and whose other
   attributes come from the arguments.

### 28.2 Code objects

A code object (%CodeType%, `__name__` `"code"`) wraps a Code Record (6.2.7).
It is immutable: assigning any attribute raises `AttributeError` ("readonly
attribute").

| Attribute | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `co_name`, `co_qualname` | the name and qualified name |
| `co_filename`, `co_firstlineno` | the file name and the first line |
| `co_argcount`, `co_posonlyargcount`, `co_kwonlyargcount` | the numbers of positional (including positional-only), positional-only, and keyword-only parameters |
| `co_varnames` | the names of the parameters (positional, keyword-only, then `*args`, then `**kwargs`) followed by the other local names, in order of first appearance |
| `co_nlocals` | the length of `co_varnames` |
| `co_cellvars`, `co_freevars` | the **cell** names and the **free** names (8.4), in order of first appearance |
| `co_names` | the global, builtin, and attribute names the code uses, in order of first use |
| `co_consts` | a tuple whose first element is the docstring when the code has one |
| `co_flags` | Table 28-1 |
| `co_code`, `co_stacksize`, `co_exceptiontable`, `co_linetable` | implementation-defined (Annex D); `co_code` is a `bytes` and `co_stacksize` a positive `int` |

Table 28-1: Code flags

| Value | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 0x01, 0x02 | always set for function code (optimized, new locals) |
| 0x04 | has `*args` |
| 0x08 | has `**kwargs` |
| 0x10 | nested in a function |
| 0x20 | generator |
| 0x80 | coroutine |
| 0x100 | iterable coroutine (27.4.5) |
| 0x200 | asynchronous generator |
| 0x4000000 | has a docstring |

Methods: `replace(**changes)` returns a copy with the named `co_` attributes
replaced; `co_lines()` returns an iterator of (*start*, *end*, *line*) triples
whose ranges are consecutive and cover `co_code` from 0; `co_positions()`
returns an iterator of (*line*, *end_line*, *col*, *end_col*) quadruples, one
per instruction unit. Code objects compare equal when all their attributes
are equal, and hash accordingly. `repr` is "\<code object *name* at *address*,
file "*filename*", line *n*\>". Calling the code type to build a code object is
implementation-defined.

### 28.3 Frame objects

A frame object (%FrameType%, `__name__` `"frame"`) exposes a Frame Record (9.2)
that is or was running. A program obtains one from a traceback's `tb_frame`, a
generator's `gi_frame`, or a host facility.

| Attribute | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `f_code`, `f_globals`, `f_builtins` | the code, the globals, the builtins namespace |
| `f_back` | the frame that called this one, or `None` (always `None` for a generator's frame) |
| `f_locals` | for a function frame, a *frame locals proxy* (`FrameLocalsProxy`), a mapping that reads and writes the frame's local variables and cells; for module and class frames, the locals mapping itself |
| `f_lineno` | the current line; assignable only from a trace function ("f\_lineno can only be set in a trace function") |
| `f_lasti` | the current instruction index (implementation-defined) |
| `f_generator` | the generator or coroutine owning the frame while it runs, else `None` |
| `f_trace`, `f_trace_lines`, `f_trace_opcodes` | host tracing hooks; the latter two accept only `bool` |

`clear()` releases the frame's local variables; on the frame of a generator
that has not started it finalizes the generator; on an executing or suspended
frame it raises a `RuntimeError` exception ("cannot clear an executing frame"
or "cannot clear a suspended frame"). `repr` is "\<frame at *address*, file
'*filename*', line *n*, code *name*\>".

### 28.4 Method objects

A method object (%MethodType%, `__name__` `"method"`) binds a callable
`__func__` to an object `__self__`. `method(function, instance)` requires a
callable ("first argument must be callable") and an instance that is not
`None` ("instance must not be None").

Calling a method calls `__func__` with `__self__` inserted before the
arguments (7.4.1). Attribute reads that the method type does not define are
forwarded to `__func__`, so `__name__`, `__qualname__`, `__doc__`,
`__module__`, and function attributes read through; setting an attribute
raises `AttributeError` ("'method' object has no attribute '*name*' and no
\_\_dict\_\_ for setting new attributes"). Two methods are equal when their
`__func__` are equal and their `__self__` are the same object; the hash
combines the hash of `__func__` with the identity of `__self__`. `repr` is
"\<bound method *qualname* of *repr*(`__self__`)\>". A method's `__get__`
returns the method itself.

### 28.5 Built-in functions and methods

%BuiltinFunctionType% (`__name__` `"builtin_function_or_method"`) is the type
of functions implemented by the implementation: the functions of clause 19
and the methods of built-in objects bound to their instance.

| Attribute | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `__name__`, `__qualname__` | the name; for a bound method *T*`.`*name* |
| `__self__` | the object the method is bound to; for a module-level built-in function, its module (the module object of `builtins`) |
| `__module__` | the module name for a function, `None` for a bound method |
| `__doc__`, `__text_signature__` | implementation-defined text, or `None` |

They are not descriptors: storing one in a class does not make it a method.
Two are equal when they wrap the same function and the same `__self__`.
`repr` is "\<built-in function *name*\>" or "\<built-in method *name* of
*tname*(`__self__`) object at *address*\>".

### 28.6 Cell objects

A cell (%CellType%, `__name__` `"cell"`) holds at most one object. `cell()`
creates an empty cell and `cell(x)` one holding *x*. `cell_contents` reads the
object (`ValueError` "Cell is empty" when empty), and can be assigned and
deleted. Cells compare by their contents (an empty cell is less than a full
one and equal to another empty one) and are unhashable. `repr` is "\<cell at
*address*: *tname*(*x*) object at *address*\>" or "\<cell at *address*:
empty\>".

### 28.7 Method descriptors

The methods of built-in types are stored in their classes as descriptors:

| `__name__` | Kind | Bound form |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `method_descriptor` | an ordinary method, `list.append` | %BuiltinFunctionType% |
| `wrapper_descriptor` | a special method slot, `list.__add__` | `method-wrapper` |
| `classmethod_descriptor` | a class method, `dict.__dict__['fromkeys']` | %BuiltinFunctionType% bound to the class |

Each has `__name__`, `__qualname__` (*T*`.`*name*), `__objclass__` (the type
*T* defining it), and `__doc__`. `__get__(instance, owner)` returns the
descriptor itself when *instance* is `None` (for a `classmethod_descriptor`,
binds *owner*) and otherwise the bound form; the instance shall be an instance
of `__objclass__` ("descriptor '*name*' for '*T*' objects doesn't apply to a
'*tname*' object"). Calling the descriptor directly takes the instance as the
first argument, with the same check (for a slot wrapper, "descriptor '*name*'
requires a '*T*' object but received a '*tname*'"); a call without arguments
raises `TypeError` ("unbound method *T*.*name*() needs an argument", or
"descriptor '*name*' of '*T*' object needs an argument").

The reprs are "\<method '*name*' of '*T*' objects\>", "\<slot wrapper '*name*'
of '*T*' objects\>", "\<method '*name*' of '*T*' objects\>", and, for a bound
`method-wrapper`, "\<method-wrapper '*name*' of *tname* object at
*address*\>".

### 28.8 Getset and member descriptors

%GetSetDescriptorType% (`getset_descriptor`) describes a computed attribute of
a built-in type (`int.real`, `type.__name__`, `BaseException.args`, an
instance's `__dict__` slot), and %MemberDescriptorType% (`member_descriptor`) a
stored attribute (a `__slots__` entry, 10.1.2). Both are data descriptors with
`__name__`, `__qualname__`, `__objclass__`, and `__doc__`; `__get__` applies
the same instance check as 28.7. A read-only one raises `AttributeError` on
`__set__` and `__delete__` ("attribute '*name*' of '*T*' objects is not
writable"). Reading an empty slot raises `AttributeError` ("'*fqname*(*o*)'
object has no attribute '*name*'"). Reprs are "\<attribute '*name*' of '*T*'
objects\>" and "\<member '*name*' of '*T*' objects\>".

### 28.9 Traceback objects

A traceback object (%TracebackType%, `__name__` `"traceback"`) records one
level of the unwinding of an exception (18.1.3).

| Attribute | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `tb_frame` | the frame of that level (read-only) |
| `tb_lineno` | the line at which the exception passed through the level (read-only) |
| `tb_lasti` | the instruction index (implementation-defined, read-only) |
| `tb_next` | the next level towards where the exception was raised, or `None`; assignable to a traceback or `None` ("expected traceback object or None, got '*tname*'"), refusing a value that would make a cycle (`ValueError` "traceback loop detected") |

`traceback(tb_next, tb_frame, tb_lasti, tb_lineno)` creates one: *tb_next*
shall be a traceback or `None` ("expected traceback object or None, got
'*tname*'") and *tb_frame* a frame ("traceback() argument 'tb_frame' must be
frame, not *tname*").

## 29 Typing Objects

### 29.1 Overview

The language creates the objects of this clause for type parameter lists
(16.7), the `type` statement (14.15), the `|` operator on types, subscription of
built-in classes, and lazy imports. They are intrinsics. Those whose
`__module__` is `"typing"` or `"types"` are reachable by name only through the
library facade (Annex C), and by `type()` of an instance otherwise; none is an
acceptable base type except %Generic%.

The intrinsic %NoDefault% is a unique object, `repr` `typing.NoDefault`,
of the type `NoDefaultType`, that marks a type parameter without a default.

### 29.2 TypeVar

%TypeVar% (`typing.TypeVar`) objects are type variables.

#### 29.2.1 TypeVar ( *name*, ...*constraints*, *bound*=None, *covariant*=False, *contravariant*=False, *infer_variance*=False, *default*=%NoDefault% )

1. *name* shall be a `str` ("typevar() argument 'name' must be str, not
   *tname*").
2. Exactly one constraint raises `TypeError` ("A single constraint is not
   allowed"); constraints together with a *bound* raise `TypeError`
   ("Constraints cannot be combined with bound=...").
3. Both *covariant* and *contravariant* raise `ValueError` ("Bivariant types
   are not supported."); either with *infer_variance* raises `ValueError`
   ("Variance cannot be specified with infer\_variance.").
4. Return a new TypeVar whose `__module__` is the `__name__` of the caller's
   globals.

A TypeVar created by a type parameter list has `__infer_variance__` `True`,
and its bound, constraints, and default are evaluated lazily (16.7).

#### 29.2.2 Attributes and methods

`__name__`, `__bound__` (or `None`), `__constraints__` (a tuple),
`__default__` (or %NoDefault%), `__covariant__`, `__contravariant__`, and
`__infer_variance__` are read-only ("readonly attribute"). `has_default()`
says whether a default was given. `repr` is the name prefixed by `+` for a
covariant, `-` for a contravariant, `~` for an invariant variable, and nothing
when the variance is inferred. `T | X` and `X | T` make a union (29.5). A
TypeVar used as a base class raises `TypeError` ("Cannot subclass an instance
of TypeVar"). `__typing_subst__(arg)` returns *arg* (after the library's
checks); `__reduce__()` returns the name.

### 29.3 TypeVarTuple

%TypeVarTuple% (`typing.TypeVarTuple`) objects, created by `*Ts` or
`TypeVarTuple(name, *, default=NoDefault)`, have `__name__`, `__default__`,
and `has_default()`. `repr` is the name. Iterating over one yields a single
`typing.Unpack[Ts]` object (a generic alias whose origin is the library's
`Unpack`; Annex C), so that `*Ts` in a subscription stands for the unpacked
tuple.

### 29.4 ParamSpec

%ParamSpec% (`typing.ParamSpec`) objects, created by `**P` or
`ParamSpec(name, *, bound=None, covariant=False, contravariant=False,
infer_variance=False, default=NoDefault)`, have the attributes of a TypeVar
that apply. `P.args` and `P.kwargs` are `ParamSpecArgs` and `ParamSpecKwargs`
objects whose `__origin__` is *P*, with reprs "*P*.args" and "*P*.kwargs".

### 29.5 Union types

%UnionType% (`typing.Union`, `repr` "\<class 'typing.Union'\>") objects
represent `X | Y`. Calling the type raises `TypeError` ("cannot create
'typing.Union' instances"); subscripting the type itself, `Union[X, Y]`,
returns ? MakeUnion over the arguments.

#### 29.5.1 MakeUnion ( *x*, *y* )

1. Each operand shall be a *union member*: a class, `None`, a union, a generic
   alias, a TypeVar, ParamSpec, or TypeAliasType, or another object whose type
   defines `__origin__`; otherwise MakeUnion returns `NotImplemented` (so `int
   | 1` raises "unsupported operand type(s) for |: 'type' and 'int'").
2. Let *args* be the members of *x* followed by those of *y*, where a union
   contributes its `__args__` and `None` contributes `type(None)`.
3. Remove every element equal to an earlier one.
4. If one element remains, return it.
5. Return a new union with `__args__` *args*.

#### 29.5.2 Operations

| Operation | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `__args__` | the members, as a tuple |
| `__parameters__` | the type parameters occurring in the members, in order |
| `u[…]` | substitution of `__parameters__`; a union without parameters raises `TypeError` ("*repr*(*u*) is not a generic class") |
| `==`, `hash` | equality of the sets of members, ignoring order |
| `isinstance(o, u)`, `issubclass(c, u)` | whether some member accepts it; a member that is a parameterized generic raises `TypeError` ("isinstance() argument 2 cannot be a parameterized generic") |
| `u \| x`, `x \| u` | MakeUnion |
| `repr(u)` | the members' type reprs (29.9.3) joined with `" \| "`, `None` for `type(None)` |

### 29.6 TypeAliasType

%TypeAliasType% (`typing.TypeAliasType`) objects are created by the `type`
statement (14.15) or by `TypeAliasType(name, value, *, type_params=())`
(*name* a `str`, "typealias() argument 'name' must be str, not *tname*";
*value* required, "typealias() missing required argument 'value' (pos 2)";
each type parameter a TypeVar, TypeVarTuple, or ParamSpec, "Expected a type
param, got *repr*").

| Attribute | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `__name__` | the name |
| `__module__` | the `__name__` of the defining globals (writable) |
| `__value__` | the value; for an alias made by the `type` statement, the value expression is evaluated in its annotation scope the first time `__value__` is read, and the result kept |
| `__type_params__`, `__parameters__` | the type parameters |

The attributes other than `__module__` are read-only ("attribute '*name*' of
'typing.TypeAliasType' objects is not writable"). `repr` is the name.
Subscripting a generic alias returns a generic alias (29.9) whose origin is
the TypeAliasType; subscripting a non-generic one raises `TypeError` ("Only
generic type aliases are subscriptable"). It supports `|`.

### 29.7 Generic

%Generic% (`typing.Generic`) is a class. A class with a type parameter list
(16.7) gets `%Generic%[`*params*`]` appended to its bases (16.4.1); that
alias's `__mro_entries__` contributes %Generic% unless another base already
derives from it, and %Generic%'s `__init_subclass__` raises `TypeError`
("Cannot inherit from Generic[...] multiple times.") when the bases include a
second `Generic[…]`. The class's `__type_params__` and `__parameters__` are the
parameters. `Generic[T1, …]` requires type variables, TypeVarTuples, or
ParamSpecs ("Parameters to Generic[...] must all be type variables or parameter
specification variables.") and returns a generic alias usable as a base,
which sets `__parameters__` of the derived class. Calling `Generic()` creates an instance as `object()` does.

Subscripting a generic class *C* (through `Generic.__class_getitem__`) checks
the number of arguments against `__parameters__` ("Too many arguments for
*repr*(*C*); actual *m*, expected *n*", or "Too few …") and returns a generic
alias (29.9) with origin *C*. The library's `Protocol` and the other `typing`
forms are not part of this Standard (Annex C).

### 29.8 LazyImportType

%LazyImportType% (`__name__` `"lazy_import"`, module `builtins`) is the type of
the placeholders bound by lazy imports (17.5). It cannot be instantiated
("cannot create 'lazy\_import' instances"). A placeholder's `repr` is
"\<lazy\_import '*name*'\>", where *name* is the module name, followed by
`.`*attr* for a `from` import. Its `resolve()` method performs the import the
statement would have performed and returns the result without rebinding the
name.

When reification or `resolve()` fails with an exception *e*, *e* is raised
with its `__cause__` set to a new `ImportError` exception ("lazy import of
'*name*' raised an exception during resolution") whose traceback refers to the
lazy import statement.

### 29.9 Generic aliases

#### 29.9.1 GenericAlias objects

%GenericAlias% (`types.GenericAlias`) objects represent a subscripted class.
`GenericAlias(origin, args)` takes exactly two positional arguments
("GenericAlias expected 2 arguments, got *n*"; "GenericAlias() takes no keyword
arguments"); a non-tuple *args* is wrapped in a tuple.
The built-in classes `tuple`, `list`, `dict`, `frozendict`, `set`,
`frozenset`, `enumerate`, `slice`, `memoryview`, `classmethod`,
`staticmethod`, `BaseExceptionGroup`, `ExceptionGroup`, and %Template% define
`__class_getitem__` returning a GenericAlias, and subscripting `type` itself
returns one.

| Member | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `__origin__`, `__args__` | the class and the arguments |
| `__parameters__` | the type parameters in `__args__` (also inside nested aliases), in order of first appearance |
| `__unpacked__` | whether the alias is `*tuple[…]` |
| other attributes | read from `__origin__`, except the alias's own and dunder names other than `__name__`, `__qualname__`, `__module__`, `__doc__` |
| calling | calls `__origin__` with the arguments and sets `__orig_class__` on the result when possible |
| `__mro_entries__(bases)` | (`__origin__`,), so the alias can be a base class; the class records the alias in `__orig_bases__` |
| `==`, `hash` | by origin, arguments, and unpacked flag |
| `a[…]` | substitution (29.9.2) |
| `iter(a)` | yields one alias equal to `*a` |
| `isinstance`, `issubclass` | `TypeError` ("isinstance() argument 2 cannot be a parameterized generic") |

#### 29.9.2 Substitution

`a[args]` with *params* = `a.__parameters__`:

1. If *params* is empty, raise `TypeError` ("*repr*(*a*) is not a generic
   class").
2. Let *args* be a tuple (a single argument is wrapped). Match *args* to
   *params*, a TypeVarTuple taking the surplus arguments; a count mismatch
   raises `TypeError` ("Too many arguments for *repr*(*a*); actual *m*,
   expected *n*" or "Too few …").
3. Return a new alias of the same origin whose arguments are `__args__` with
   each parameter replaced by its argument, recursively inside nested aliases
   and unions.

#### 29.9.3 Representation

`repr(a)` is the origin's type repr followed by `[`, the arguments' type reprs
separated by `", "`, and `]` (`[()]` for no arguments), prefixed by `*` when
unpacked. The *type repr* of an object is: `...` for `Ellipsis`; the
qualified name (with the module unless `builtins`) for a class, and `None`
for `type(None)`; `repr` for a
generic alias, union, type variable, or string; a list's type reprs in
brackets; and `repr` otherwise.

#### 29.9.4 `__class_getitem__`

Subscripting a class *C* that is not handled by its metaclass's `__getitem__`
calls *C*.`__class_getitem__` (7.8), which is implicitly a class method when
defined in a class body (16.4.3).

## Annex A (normative) Grammar Summary

This annex collects the grammar of clauses 12 through 17 in one place, in the
notation of 5.1.1. Where a rule here and a rule in the body of this Standard
differ, the body governs. The early errors that refine the grammar are given
with each construct and are not repeated.

### A.1 Lexical grammar

The token kinds are `NAME`, `NUMBER`, `STRING`, `FSTRING_START`,
`FSTRING_MIDDLE`, `FSTRING_END`, `TSTRING_START`, `TSTRING_MIDDLE`,
`TSTRING_END`, the operators and delimiters of 12.7, `NEWLINE`, `INDENT`,
`DEDENT`, and `ENDMARKER` (12.1); the f-string and t-string parts are
produced by the tokenization of 12.5.6 and 12.5.7.

The keywords (12.4.2) are `False`, `None`, `True`, `and`, `as`, `assert`,
`async`, `await`, `break`, `class`, `continue`, `def`, `del`, `elif`, `else`,
`except`, `finally`, `for`, `from`, `global`, `if`, `import`, `in`, `is`,
`lambda`, `nonlocal`, `not`, `or`, `pass`, `raise`, `return`, `try`, `while`,
`with`, and `yield`; the soft keywords are `match`, `case`, `_`, `type`, and
`lazy`. The operators and delimiters are those listed in 12.7.

```text
identifier:   id_start id_continue*
id_start:     <"_" or a code point with the Unicode property XID_Start>
id_continue:  <id_start, or a code point with the property XID_Continue>

stringliteral:   [stringprefix] (shortstring | longstring)
stringprefix:    "r" | "u" | "b" | "br" | "rb" | "f" | "fr" | "rf" | "t" | "tr" | "rt"
                 (any letter may be uppercase)
shortstring:     "'" shortchar* "'" | '"' shortchar* '"'
longstring:      "'''" longchar* "'''" | '"""' longchar* '"""'
shortchar:       <any code point except "\", the line terminators, and the quote>
                 | escapeseq
longchar:        <any code point except "\"> | escapeseq
escapeseq:       "\" <any code point>

fstring:            fstring_start fstring_part* fstring_end
fstring_part:       fstring_middle | "{{" | "}}" | replacement_field
replacement_field:  "{" annotated_expr ["="] ["!" conversion] [":" format_spec] "}"
annotated_expr:     star_expressions | yield_expr
conversion:         "s" | "r" | "a"
format_spec:        (fstring_middle | "{{" | "}}" | replacement_field)*

integer:      decinteger | bininteger | octinteger | hexinteger
decinteger:   nonzerodigit (["_"] digit)* | "0"+ (["_"] "0")*
bininteger:   "0" ("b" | "B") (["_"] bindigit)+
octinteger:   "0" ("o" | "O") (["_"] octdigit)+
hexinteger:   "0" ("x" | "X") (["_"] hexdigit)+
nonzerodigit: "1"..."9"
digit:        "0"..."9"
bindigit:     "0" | "1"
octdigit:     "0"..."7"
hexdigit:     digit | "a"..."f" | "A"..."F"

floatnumber:   pointfloat | exponentfloat
pointfloat:    [digitpart] fraction | digitpart "."
exponentfloat: (digitpart | pointfloat) exponent
digitpart:     digit (["_"] digit)*
fraction:      "." digitpart
exponent:      ("e" | "E") ["+" | "-"] digitpart
imagnumber:    (floatnumber | digitpart) ("j" | "J")
```

### A.2 Top-level components

```text
file: [statements] ENDMARKER
interactive: statement_newline
eval: expressions NEWLINE* ENDMARKER
statements: statement+
statement:
    | compound_stmt
    | simple_stmts
single_compound_stmt:
    | compound_stmt
statement_newline:
    | single_compound_stmt NEWLINE
    | simple_stmts
    | NEWLINE
    | ENDMARKER
simple_stmts:
    | simple_stmt !';' NEWLINE
    | ';'.simple_stmt+ [';'] NEWLINE
```

### A.3 Simple statements

```text
simple_stmt:
    | assignment
    | &"type" type_alias
    | &('import' | 'from' | "lazy") import_stmt
    | star_expressions
    | &'return' return_stmt
    | &'raise' raise_stmt
    | &'pass' pass_stmt
    | &'del' del_stmt
    | &'yield' yield_stmt
    | &'assert' assert_stmt
    | &'break' break_stmt
    | &'continue' continue_stmt
    | &'global' global_stmt
    | &'nonlocal' nonlocal_stmt
assignment:
    | NAME ':' expression ['=' annotated_rhs ]
    | ('(' single_target ')'
         | single_subscript_attribute_target) ':' expression ['=' annotated_rhs ]
    | (star_targets '=' )+ annotated_rhs !'='
    | single_target augassign ~ annotated_rhs
annotated_rhs: yield_expr | star_expressions
augassign:
    | '+='
    | '-='
    | '*='
    | '@='
    | '/='
    | '%='
    | '&='
    | '|='
    | '^='
    | '<<='
    | '>>='
    | '**='
    | '//='
return_stmt:
    | 'return' [star_expressions]
raise_stmt:
    | 'raise' expression 'from' expression
    | 'raise' expression
    | 'raise'
pass_stmt:
    | 'pass'
break_stmt:
    | 'break'
continue_stmt:
    | 'continue'
global_stmt: 'global' ','.NAME+
nonlocal_stmt: 'nonlocal' ','.NAME+
del_stmt:
    | 'del' del_targets &(';' | NEWLINE)
yield_stmt: yield_expr
assert_stmt:
    | 'assert' expression [',' expression ]
import_stmt:
    | import_name
    | import_from
import_name:
    | "lazy"? 'import' dotted_as_names
import_from:
    | "lazy"? 'from' ('.' | '...')* dotted_name 'import' import_from_targets
    | "lazy"? 'from' ('.' | '...')+ 'import' import_from_targets
import_from_targets:
    | '(' import_from_as_names [','] ')'
    | import_from_as_names !','
    | '*'
import_from_as_names:
    | ','.import_from_as_name+
import_from_as_name:
    | NAME ['as' NAME ]
dotted_as_names:
    | ','.dotted_as_name+
dotted_as_name:
    | dotted_name ['as' NAME ]
dotted_name:
    | dotted_name '.' NAME
    | NAME
```

### A.4 Compound statements

```text
compound_stmt:
    | &('def' | '@' | 'async') function_def
    | &'if' if_stmt
    | &('class' | '@') class_def
    | &('with' | 'async') with_stmt
    | &('for' | 'async') for_stmt
    | &'try' try_stmt
    | &'while' while_stmt
    | match_stmt
block:
    | NEWLINE INDENT statements DEDENT
    | simple_stmts
if_stmt:
    | 'if' named_expression ':' block elif_stmt
    | 'if' named_expression ':' block [else_block]
elif_stmt:
    | 'elif' named_expression ':' block elif_stmt
    | 'elif' named_expression ':' block [else_block]
else_block:
    | 'else' &&':' block
while_stmt:
    | 'while' named_expression ':' block [else_block]
for_stmt:
    | 'for' star_targets 'in' ~ star_expressions ':' block [else_block]
    | 'async' 'for' star_targets 'in' ~ star_expressions ':' block [else_block]
with_stmt:
    | 'with' '(' ','.with_item+ ','? ')' ':' block
    | 'with' ','.with_item+ ':' block
    | 'async' 'with' '(' ','.with_item+ ','? ')' ':' block
    | 'async' 'with' ','.with_item+ ':' block
with_item:
    | expression 'as' star_target &(',' | ')' | ':')
    | expression
try_stmt:
    | 'try' &&':' block finally_block
    | 'try' &&':' block except_block+ [else_block] [finally_block]
    | 'try' &&':' block except_star_block+ [else_block] [finally_block]
except_block:
    | 'except' expression ':' block
    | 'except' expression 'as' NAME ':' block
    | 'except' expressions ':' block
    | 'except' ':' block
except_star_block:
    | 'except' '*' expression ':' block
    | 'except' '*' expression 'as' NAME ':' block
    | 'except' '*' expressions ':' block
finally_block:
    | 'finally' &&':' block
match_stmt:
    | "match" subject_expr ':' NEWLINE INDENT case_block+ DEDENT
subject_expr:
    | star_named_expression ',' star_named_expressions?
    | named_expression
case_block:
    | "case" patterns guard? ':' block
guard: 'if' named_expression
```

### A.5 Function and class definitions

```text
decorators: ('@' named_expression NEWLINE )+
class_def:
    | decorators class_def_raw
    | class_def_raw
class_def_raw:
    | 'class' NAME [type_params] ['(' [arguments] ')' ] ':' block
function_def:
    | decorators function_def_raw
    | function_def_raw
function_def_raw:
    | 'def' NAME [type_params] '(' [params] ')' ['->' expression ] ':' block
    | 'async' 'def' NAME [type_params] '(' [params] ')' ['->' expression ] ':' block
params:
    | parameters
parameters:
    | slash_no_default param_no_default* param_with_default* [star_etc]
    | slash_with_default param_with_default* [star_etc]
    | param_no_default+ param_with_default* [star_etc]
    | param_with_default+ [star_etc]
    | star_etc
slash_no_default:
    | param_no_default+ '/' ','
    | param_no_default+ '/' &')'
slash_with_default:
    | param_no_default* param_with_default+ '/' ','
    | param_no_default* param_with_default+ '/' &')'
star_etc:
    | '*' param_no_default param_maybe_default* [kwds]
    | '*' param_no_default_star_annotation param_maybe_default* [kwds]
    | '*' ',' param_maybe_default+ [kwds]
    | kwds
kwds:
    | '**' param_no_default
param_no_default:
    | param ','?
    | param? &')'
param_no_default_star_annotation:
    | param_star_annotation ','?
    | param_star_annotation? &')'
param_with_default:
    | param default ','?
    | param default? &')'
param_maybe_default:
    | param default? ','?
    | param default?? &')'
param: NAME annotation?
param_star_annotation: NAME star_annotation
annotation: ':' expression
star_annotation: ':' star_expression
type_alias:
    | "type" NAME [type_params] '=' expression
type_params:
    | '[' type_param_seq ']'
type_param_seq: ','.type_param+ [',']
type_param:
    | NAME [type_param_bound] [type_param_default]
    | '*' NAME [type_param_starred_default]
    | '**' NAME [type_param_default]
type_param_bound: ':' expression
type_param_default: '=' expression
type_param_starred_default: '=' star_expression
```

### A.6 Expressions

```text
expressions:
    | expression (',' expression )+ [',']
    | expression ','
    | expression
expression:
    | if_expression
    | disjunction
    | lambdef
if_expression:
    | disjunction 'if' disjunction 'else' expression
yield_expr:
    | 'yield' 'from' expression
    | 'yield' [star_expressions]
star_expressions:
    | star_expression (',' star_expression )+ [',']
    | star_expression ','
    | star_expression
star_expression:
    | '*' bitwise_or
    | expression
star_named_expressions: ','.star_named_expression+ [',']
star_named_expressions_sequence: ','.star_named_expression_sequence+ [',']
star_named_expression:
    | '*' bitwise_or
    | named_expression
star_named_expression_sequence:
    | star_named_expression
assignment_expression:
    | NAME ':=' ~ expression
named_expression:
    | assignment_expression
    | expression !':='
disjunction:
    | conjunction ('or' conjunction )+
    | conjunction
conjunction:
    | inversion ('and' inversion )+
    | inversion
inversion:
    | 'not' inversion
    | comparison
comparison:
    | bitwise_or compare_op_bitwise_or_pair+
    | bitwise_or
compare_op_bitwise_or_pair:
    | eq_bitwise_or
    | noteq_bitwise_or
    | lte_bitwise_or
    | lt_bitwise_or
    | gte_bitwise_or
    | gt_bitwise_or
    | notin_bitwise_or
    | in_bitwise_or
    | isnot_bitwise_or
    | is_bitwise_or
eq_bitwise_or: '==' bitwise_or
noteq_bitwise_or:
    | ('!=' ) bitwise_or
lte_bitwise_or: '<=' bitwise_or
lt_bitwise_or: '<' bitwise_or
gte_bitwise_or: '>=' bitwise_or
gt_bitwise_or: '>' bitwise_or
notin_bitwise_or: 'not' 'in' bitwise_or
in_bitwise_or: 'in' bitwise_or
isnot_bitwise_or: 'is' 'not' bitwise_or
is_bitwise_or: 'is' bitwise_or
bitwise_or:
    | bitwise_or '|' bitwise_xor
    | bitwise_xor
bitwise_xor:
    | bitwise_xor '^' bitwise_and
    | bitwise_and
bitwise_and:
    | bitwise_and '&' shift_expr
    | shift_expr
shift_expr:
    | shift_expr '<<' sum
    | shift_expr '>>' sum
    | sum
sum:
    | sum '+' term
    | sum '-' term
    | term
term:
    | term '*' factor
    | term '/' factor
    | term '//' factor
    | term '%' factor
    | term '@' factor
    | factor
factor:
    | '+' factor
    | '-' factor
    | '~' factor
    | power
power:
    | await_primary '**' factor
    | await_primary
await_primary:
    | 'await' primary
    | primary
primary:
    | primary '.' NAME
    | primary genexp
    | primary '(' [arguments] ')'
    | primary '[' slices ']'
    | atom
slices:
    | slice !','
    | ','.(slice | starred_expression)+ [',']
slice:
    | [expression] ':' [expression] [':' [expression] ]
    | named_expression
atom:
    | NAME
    | 'True'
    | 'False'
    | 'None'
    | &(STRING|FSTRING_START|TSTRING_START) strings
    | NUMBER
    | &'(' (genexp | tuple | group)
    | &'[' (listcomp | list)
    | &'{' (dictcomp | setcomp | dict | set)
    | '...'
group:
    | '(' (yield_expr | named_expression) ')'
lambdef:
    | 'lambda' [lambda_params] ':' expression
lambda_params:
    | lambda_parameters
lambda_parameters:
    | lambda_slash_no_default lambda_param_no_default* lambda_param_with_default* [lambda_star_etc]
    | lambda_slash_with_default lambda_param_with_default* [lambda_star_etc]
    | lambda_param_no_default+ lambda_param_with_default* [lambda_star_etc]
    | lambda_param_with_default+ [lambda_star_etc]
    | lambda_star_etc
lambda_slash_no_default:
    | lambda_param_no_default+ '/' ','
    | lambda_param_no_default+ '/' &':'
lambda_slash_with_default:
    | lambda_param_no_default* lambda_param_with_default+ '/' ','
    | lambda_param_no_default* lambda_param_with_default+ '/' &':'
lambda_star_etc:
    | '*' lambda_param_no_default lambda_param_maybe_default* [lambda_kwds]
    | '*' ',' lambda_param_maybe_default+ [lambda_kwds]
    | lambda_kwds
lambda_kwds:
    | '**' lambda_param_no_default
lambda_param_no_default:
    | lambda_param ','
    | lambda_param &':'
lambda_param_with_default:
    | lambda_param default ','
    | lambda_param default &':'
lambda_param_maybe_default:
    | lambda_param default? ','
    | lambda_param default? &':'
lambda_param: NAME
string: STRING
strings:
    | (fstring|string)+
    | tstring+
list:
    | '[' [star_named_expressions_sequence] ']'
tuple:
    | '(' [star_named_expression_sequence ',' [star_named_expressions_sequence]  ] ')'
set: '{' star_named_expressions_sequence '}'
dict:
    | '{' [double_starred_kvpairs] '}'
double_starred_kvpairs: ','.double_starred_kvpair+ [',']
double_starred_kvpair:
    | '**' bitwise_or
    | kvpair
kvpair: expression ':' expression
for_if_clauses:
    | for_if_clause+
for_if_clause:
    | 'async' 'for' star_targets 'in' ~ disjunction ('if' disjunction )*
    | 'for' star_targets 'in' ~ disjunction ('if' disjunction )*
listcomp:
    | '[' star_named_expression for_if_clauses ']'
setcomp:
    | '{' star_named_expression for_if_clauses '}'
genexp:
    | '(' ( assignment_expression | expression !':=' | starred_expression ) for_if_clauses ')'
dictcomp:
    | '{' kvpair for_if_clauses '}'
    | '{' '**' expression for_if_clauses '}'
arguments:
    | args [','] &')'
args:
    | ','.(starred_expression | ( assignment_expression | expression !':=') !'=')+ [',' kwargs ]
    | kwargs
kwargs:
    | ','.kwarg_or_starred+ ',' ','.kwarg_or_double_starred+
    | ','.kwarg_or_starred+
    | ','.kwarg_or_double_starred+
starred_expression:
    | '*' expression
kwarg_or_starred:
    | NAME '=' expression
    | starred_expression
kwarg_or_double_starred:
    | NAME '=' expression
    | '**' expression
```

### A.7 f-strings and t-strings

```text
fstring_middle:
    | fstring_replacement_field
    | FSTRING_MIDDLE
fstring_replacement_field:
    | '{' annotated_rhs '='? [fstring_conversion] [fstring_full_format_spec] '}'
fstring_conversion:
    | "!" NAME
fstring_full_format_spec:
    | ':' fstring_format_spec*
fstring_format_spec:
    | FSTRING_MIDDLE
    | fstring_replacement_field
fstring:
    | FSTRING_START fstring_middle* FSTRING_END
tstring_format_spec_replacement_field:
    | '{' annotated_rhs '='? [fstring_conversion] [tstring_full_format_spec] '}'
tstring_format_spec:
    | TSTRING_MIDDLE
    | tstring_format_spec_replacement_field
tstring_full_format_spec:
    | ':' tstring_format_spec*
tstring_replacement_field:
    | '{' annotated_rhs '='? [fstring_conversion] [tstring_full_format_spec] '}'
tstring_middle:
    | tstring_replacement_field
    | TSTRING_MIDDLE
tstring:
    | TSTRING_START tstring_middle* TSTRING_END
```

### A.8 Targets

```text
star_targets:
    | star_target !','
    | star_target (',' star_target )* [',']
star_targets_list_seq: ','.star_target+ [',']
star_targets_tuple_seq:
    | star_target (',' star_target )+ [',']
    | star_target ','
star_target:
    | '*' (!'*' star_target)
    | target_with_star_atom
target_with_star_atom:
    | t_primary '.' NAME !t_lookahead
    | t_primary '[' slices ']' !t_lookahead
    | star_atom
star_atom:
    | NAME
    | '(' target_with_star_atom ')'
    | '(' [star_targets_tuple_seq] ')'
    | '[' [star_targets_list_seq] ']'
single_target:
    | single_subscript_attribute_target
    | NAME
    | '(' single_target ')'
single_subscript_attribute_target:
    | t_primary '.' NAME !t_lookahead
    | t_primary '[' slices ']' !t_lookahead
t_primary:
    | t_primary '.' NAME &t_lookahead
    | t_primary '[' slices ']' &t_lookahead
    | t_primary genexp &t_lookahead
    | t_primary '(' [arguments] ')' &t_lookahead
    | atom &t_lookahead
t_lookahead: '(' | '[' | '.'
del_targets: ','.del_target+ [',']
del_target:
    | t_primary '.' NAME !t_lookahead
    | t_primary '[' slices ']' !t_lookahead
    | del_t_atom
del_t_atom:
    | NAME
    | '(' del_target ')'
    | '(' [del_targets] ')'
    | '[' [del_targets] ']'
```

### A.9 Patterns

```text
patterns:
    | open_sequence_pattern
    | pattern
pattern:
    | as_pattern
    | or_pattern
as_pattern:
    | or_pattern 'as' pattern_capture_target
or_pattern:
    | '|'.closed_pattern+
closed_pattern:
    | literal_pattern
    | capture_pattern
    | wildcard_pattern
    | value_pattern
    | group_pattern
    | sequence_pattern
    | mapping_pattern
    | class_pattern
literal_pattern:
    | signed_number !('+' | '-')
    | complex_number
    | strings
    | 'None'
    | 'True'
    | 'False'
literal_expr:
    | signed_number !('+' | '-')
    | complex_number
    | &(STRING|FSTRING_START|TSTRING_START) strings
    | 'None'
    | 'True'
    | 'False'
complex_number:
    | signed_real_number '+' imaginary_number
    | signed_real_number '-' imaginary_number
signed_number:
    | NUMBER
    | '+' NUMBER
    | '-' NUMBER
signed_real_number:
    | real_number
    | '+' real_number
    | '-' real_number
real_number:
    | NUMBER
imaginary_number:
    | NUMBER
capture_pattern:
    | pattern_capture_target
pattern_capture_target:
    | !"_" NAME !('.' | '(' | '=')
wildcard_pattern:
    | "_"
value_pattern:
    | attr !('.' | '(' | '=')
attr:
    | name_or_attr '.' NAME
name_or_attr:
    | attr
    | NAME
group_pattern:
    | '(' pattern ')'
sequence_pattern:
    | '[' maybe_sequence_pattern? ']'
    | '(' open_sequence_pattern? ')'
open_sequence_pattern:
    | maybe_star_pattern ',' maybe_sequence_pattern?
maybe_sequence_pattern:
    | ','.maybe_star_pattern+ ','?
maybe_star_pattern:
    | star_pattern
    | pattern
star_pattern:
    | '*' pattern_capture_target
    | '*' wildcard_pattern
mapping_pattern:
    | '{' '}'
    | '{' double_star_pattern ','? '}'
    | '{' items_pattern ',' double_star_pattern ','? '}'
    | '{' items_pattern ','? '}'
items_pattern:
    | ','.key_value_pattern+
key_value_pattern:
    | (literal_expr | attr) ':' pattern
double_star_pattern:
    | '**' pattern_capture_target
class_pattern:
    | name_or_attr '(' ')'
    | name_or_attr '(' positional_patterns ','? ')'
    | name_or_attr '(' keyword_patterns ','? ')'
    | name_or_attr '(' positional_patterns ',' keyword_patterns ','? ')'
positional_patterns:
    | ','.pattern+
keyword_patterns:
    | ','.keyword_pattern+
keyword_pattern:
    | NAME '=' pattern
```

## Annex B (normative) Host Hooks

A *host hook* is an implementation-supplied operation through which a language
construct reaches a facility outside the language. A Core implementation need
supply none of them; each subclause states the behaviour when the hook is
absent. A Host implementation documents the hooks it supplies. A hook may call
back into the language (to run code, raise exceptions, or call `write`
methods); it shall return a normal completion with a value of the stated kind
or a raise completion.

### B.1 Modules

#### B.1.1 HostFindModule ( *name*, *path* )

Locates the module named by the absolute dotted *name*; *path* is the parent
package's `__path__`, or **absent** for a top-level module. Returns **absent**
when no module of that name exists, or a *module spec*: a record of the
attributes to give the new module (`__file__`, `__path__` for a package,
`__spec__`, `__loader__`, `__package__`) and the means to execute it. When the
hook is absent, every name not already in the module cache (17.3) is not
found.

#### B.1.2 HostExecuteModule ( *spec*, *module* )

Executes the module described by *spec* in *module*: for a module of Python
source, obtains its source text (decoded as clause 11 specifies) and evaluates
it as a `file` input (17.6.1) in *module*.`__dict__`. A host may supply modules
implemented in other ways; they shall behave as module objects (17.2).

#### B.1.3 Initial modules

Before the main module runs, the module cache holds the module `builtins`,
whose namespace is the built-in namespace (clause 19). A host may preload other
modules, including the library facades of Annex C.

### B.2 Source text

#### B.2.1 HostDecodeSource ( *bytes*, *encoding* )

Decodes source bytes declared in an encoding other than UTF-8, ISO 8859-1, or
US-ASCII (11.2). Returns the text and the canonical encoding name, or
**absent** if the host does not know the encoding (11.2 then raises the
`SyntaxError`).

#### B.2.2 HostLookupCodec ( *name* )

Returns an encoder and a decoder for an encoding not in Table 23-1, or
**absent** (23.3.1 then raises `LookupError`). The codec shall report errors
by the `UnicodeError` subclasses of 21.3.6 and apply the error handlers of
23.3.2.

### B.3 Program interaction

#### B.3.1 HostStandardOutput ( )

Returns the object to which `print` writes by default, which shall have a
`write` method taking a `str`, or **absent** (then `print` writes nothing).

#### B.3.2 HostInput ( *prompt* )

Implements `input` (19.3.20). When absent, `input` raises a `RuntimeError`
exception ("input(): lost sys.stdin").

#### B.3.3 HostOpen ( *arguments* )

Implements `open` (19.3.28), returning a file object. When absent, `open`
raises an `OSError` exception.

#### B.3.4 HostDisplay ( *value* )

Displays the value of an expression statement in interactive input (17.6.2).
The default, used when the hook is absent, writes ToRepr(*value*) and a line
terminator to HostStandardOutput() and binds `_` in the built-in namespace to
*value*.

#### B.3.5 HostBreakpoint ( *args*, *kws* )

Implements `breakpoint` (19.3.7). When absent, `breakpoint` returns `None`.

### B.4 Diagnostics

#### B.4.1 HostWarn ( *category*, *message*, *location* )

Receives a warning: a subclass of `Warning` (21.6), a message string, and the
file name and line where it was issued (for warnings issued while running, the
location of the code that triggered the operation). The host may ignore it,
display it, or raise it: in the last case it returns a raise completion with an
instance of *category* with argument *message*, which propagates from the point
where the warning was issued. When the hook is absent, warnings are ignored.
`BytesWarning` (24.1) and `ResourceWarning` are issued only when the host asks
for them.

#### B.4.2 HostReportException ( *e* )

Reports an exception that propagated out of the main module or out of code the
host ran, typically by writing a traceback display (18.1.2, 18.1.3) to the
standard error stream. When absent, nothing is reported. After it, the
program ends unsuccessfully.

#### B.4.3 HostReportUnraisable ( *e*, *context* )

Reports an exception that could not propagate (18.1.4), with an object
describing where it occurred. When absent, nothing is reported.

#### B.4.4 SystemExit

When a `SystemExit` exception *e* propagates out of the main module, the
program ends without HostReportException. Its exit status is: 0 if *e*.`code` is
`None`; *e*.`code` if it is an `int`; and otherwise 1, after writing
ToStr(*e*.`code`) to the standard error stream if the host has one. How the
status reaches the environment is host-defined.

### B.5 Asynchronous generators

#### B.5.1 HostAsyncGenHooks ( )

Returns the pair (*firstiter*, *finalizer*) of callables (or `None`) that 27.5.4
calls for asynchronous generators; the host's facade (Annex C) lets programs
install them. When absent, both are `None`.

### B.6 Execution parameters

The following values are held by the implementation and may be changed by the
host, for example through the facade of Annex C; a program of the Core profile
cannot change them otherwise:

| Parameter | Initial value | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| recursion limit | implementation-defined (Annex D), 1000 recommended | 9.6 |
| int string limit | 4300 | 22.1.8 |
| asyncgen hooks | `None`, `None` | 27.5.4 |
| optimization level | 0; when greater than 0, `assert` statements are not evaluated and `__debug__` is `False` | 14.5, 19.1 |

## Annex C (informative) Library Facades

Programs written for CPython reach many of the intrinsics of this Standard
through library modules. Those modules are not part of this Standard: a host
may supply them (B.1.3) as ordinary modules, written in Python or otherwise,
that bind names to intrinsics reachable from the language, add library
functions, and call host hooks. This annex records the correspondence so that
such a host binds each name to the object this Standard describes. Every
intrinsic below can be obtained without a library: the third column shows a
Core expression that evaluates to it.

### C.1 `types`

| Name | Intrinsic | Core expression |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `FunctionType`, `LambdaType` | %FunctionType% (28.1) | `type(lambda: None)` |
| `CodeType` | %CodeType% (28.2) | `type((lambda: None).__code__)` |
| `CellType` | %CellType% (28.6) | `type((lambda x: lambda: x)(0).__closure__[0])` |
| `MethodType` | %MethodType% (28.4) | `type(C().f)` for a class `C` with method `f` |
| `BuiltinFunctionType`, `BuiltinMethodType` | %BuiltinFunctionType% (28.5) | `type(len)` |
| `WrapperDescriptorType`, `MethodWrapperType`, `MethodDescriptorType`, `ClassMethodDescriptorType` | 28.7 | `type(object.__init__)`, `type(object().__str__)`, `type(str.join)`, `type(dict.__dict__['fromkeys'])` |
| `GetSetDescriptorType`, `MemberDescriptorType` | 28.8 | `type(int.__dict__['real'])`, `type(slice.__dict__['start'])` |
| `MappingProxyType` | %MappingProxyType% (26.6) | `type(type.__dict__)` |
| `ModuleType` | %ModuleType% (17.2) | `type(__import__('builtins'))` |
| `GeneratorType`, `CoroutineType`, `AsyncGeneratorType` | 27.2, 27.4, 27.5 | `type` of instances |
| `FrameType`, `TracebackType` | 28.3, 28.9 | `type(e.__traceback__.tb_frame)`, `type(e.__traceback__)` |
| `GenericAlias` | %GenericAlias% (29.9) | `type(list[int])` |
| `UnionType` | %UnionType% (29.5) | `type(int \| str)` |
| `NoneType`, `EllipsisType`, `NotImplementedType` | 20.4 | `type(None)`, `type(...)`, `type(NotImplemented)` |
| `LazyImportType` | %LazyImportType% (29.8) | `type(globals()['m'])` after `lazy import m` |

`types.coroutine(f)` sets the iterable-coroutine flag (0x100, Table 28-1) on a
generator function's code (27.4.5); `types.SimpleNamespace`, `new_class`,
`prepare_class`, `resolve_bases`, and `get_original_bases` are library code
built on the class-creation protocol of 16.4.

### C.2 `typing`

`TypeVar`, `TypeVarTuple`, `ParamSpec`, `ParamSpecArgs`, `ParamSpecKwargs`,
`TypeAliasType`, `Generic`, `Union`, and `NoDefault` are the intrinsics of
clause 29. `Unpack` (the origin of the alias that iterating a TypeVarTuple
produces), `Protocol`, `Annotated`, `Literal`, `Callable`, `get_type_hints`,
and the other `typing` names are library code. The library may add checks to
`__typing_subst__` and to `Generic.__class_getitem__`.

### C.3 `annotationlib`

The annotate functions of 16.6 accept the formats `VALUE` (1) and
`VALUE_WITH_FAKE_GLOBALS` (2). The library's `get_annotations`, `call_annotate_function`,
`ForwardRef`, and the `FORWARDREF` (3) and `STRING` (4) formats evaluate an
annotate function with substituted globals; they are not part of this
Standard.

### C.4 `string.templatelib`

`Template` and `Interpolation` are %Template% and %Interpolation% (23.7);
`convert(value, conversion)` applies `!s`, `!r`, or `!a` as 23.4 does.

### C.5 `sys`

| Name | Correspondence |
| --- | --- |
| `modules` | %ModuleCache% (17.3) |
| `lazy_modules` | %LazyModules% (17.5) |
| `getrecursionlimit`, `setrecursionlimit` | the recursion limit (B.6) |
| `get_int_max_str_digits`, `set_int_max_str_digits` | the int string limit (22.1.8, B.6); values below 640 other than 0 are refused |
| `set_asyncgen_hooks`, `get_asyncgen_hooks` | HostAsyncGenHooks (B.5.1) |
| `stdout`, `stdin`, `stderr` | HostStandardOutput, HostInput, the stream of HostReportException |
| `displayhook`, `excepthook`, `unraisablehook`, `breakpointhook` | HostDisplay, HostReportException, HostReportUnraisable, HostBreakpoint |
| `exception()`, `exc_info()` | the handled exception (9.5) |
| `_getframe(depth)` | the frame object (28.3) of the running frame or a caller |
| `hash_info` | `width` 64, `modulus` 2<sup>61</sup> − 1, `inf` 314159, `nan` 0, `imag` 1000003 (22.1.9) |
| `maxsize` | the largest index-size value (Annex D) |
| `flags.optimize` | the optimization level (B.6) |
| `exit(status)` | raises `SystemExit(status)` (B.4.4) |

### C.6 `abc` and `collections.abc`

`abc.ABCMeta` computes `__abstractmethods__` (20.2.8) from the attributes of a
class whose `__isabstractmethod__` is true and implements `register` and the
`__instancecheck__`/`__subclasscheck__` hooks of 19.3.21. Registering a class
with `collections.abc.Sequence` or `collections.abc.Mapping` (or inheriting
from them) designates it a sequence or a mapping for `match` (15.6.3, 15.6.4).

### C.7 `copyreg` and `pickle`

The `__reduce__` and `__reduce_ex__` results of built-in objects name library
functions such as `copyreg.__newobj__` and `copyreg._reconstructor`; their
form is not specified by this Standard beyond the cases given in clauses 21
through 27.

### C.8 Abstract syntax trees

`compile` with the flag `ast.PyCF_ONLY_AST` (0x400), and `compile` of an AST
object, are host facilities that expose the parse tree of Annex A as objects;
a Core implementation without them rejects the flag.

### C.9 `__future__`

The `__future__` module binds the feature names that 14.13 recognizes to
records of the releases in which they became optional and mandatory. Importing
it is an ordinary import; only the future statement has a language effect.

### C.10 `site` and `builtins` additions

`exit`, `quit`, `help`, `copyright`, `credits`, and `license` (19.4) are added
to the built-in namespace by the host's `site` module.

### C.11 `warnings`

The `warnings` module implements HostWarn (B.4.1): its filters decide whether a
warning is ignored, displayed once per location, or raised.

## Annex D (normative) Implementation-Defined Behaviour

A conforming implementation documents its choice for each item below. The
values given for CPython are those of a 64-bit CPython 3.15 build, which an
implementation is recommended to follow where it can.

| Item | Clause | CPython |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The index-size range, the range of `int` values ToIndexSize accepts | 7.5.4 | −2<sup>63</sup> to 2<sup>63</sup> − 1 |
| The recursion limit, and which evaluations count towards the recursion depth | 9.6, B.6 | 1000; calls of Python functions, `repr` |
| When unreachable objects are reclaimed, and the order of finalization within a group (implementation-approximated) | 9.7 | reference counting with a cycle collector |
| Identity of separately computed immutable values (including `tuple()`, `frozenset()`, and `tuple(t)`), and of `copy()` results that may return the object itself | 6.1.2, 25.2, 26.2, 26.3.1 | small integers, interned strings, and empty containers are shared |
| The identity value `id` returns and `object.__repr__` prints | 19.3.19, 20.1.3 | the address |
| Hash values of `str`, `bytes`, objects hashed by identity, and `None`; the hash width (64 or 32 bits) with the corresponding hash modulus and tuple constants | 22.1.9, 23.1, 25.2 | randomized per process for `str`/`bytes`; width 64 |
| Iteration order of sets | 26.3 | by hash table position |
| The keys a dict iterator produces after keys were removed and added at an unchanged size, before it raises | 26.1.4 | those at later hash table positions, until more than the recorded size |
| Column offsets (`offset`, `end_offset`) of syntax errors, when not `None` | 18.2.1 | the offending token |
| Names bound by a match that partially succeeded | 15.6 | the bindings made before the failure are stored |
| Extra names stored in a class namespace by class creation (beyond those of 16.4.2) | 16.4.2 | `__classdictcell__` |
| The sorting algorithm, and so the order of comparisons and the order of the list when a comparison raises | 25.1.4 | Timsort (powersort merge policy) |
| Whether assertions run by default (the optimization level) | 14.5, B.6 | enabled |
| Code object internals (`co_code`, `co_stacksize`, `co_exceptiontable`, `co_linetable`), instruction indices (`f_lasti`, `tb_lasti`), and constructing code objects | 28.2, 28.3, 28.9 | CPython bytecode |
| Documentation strings and `__text_signature__` of built-in objects | 28.5 | the CPython docstrings |
| The `__name__` of string iterators | 27.1.1 | `str_ascii_iterator` for ASCII strings, `str_iterator` otherwise |
| `__sizeof__` results | 20.1.3, 20.2 | memory size in bytes |
| The numeric values of errno names, and ERANGE | 21.3.4, 22.3.2 | the host's `<errno.h>` |
| memoryview native formats: sizes and byte order | 24.4.2 | the platform ABI |
| The limits on source nesting (parentheses, blocks, f-strings) and on compilation recursion | 18.2.3 | 200 parentheses, 20 blocks, 150 f-string levels |
| The size limit of integers (the point at which a shift raises "too many digits in integer") | 22.1.3 | 2<sup>63</sup> − 1 digits of 30 bits |
| Additional dunder attributes of objects not listed in this Standard | throughout | CPython's |

An implementation shall not use this annex to differ from a behaviour that the
body of this Standard specifies.

## Annex E (informative) Changes from Revision 1

Revision 1 (`SLP-315:2026-draft1`) consolidated the chapters of the Python
Language Reference and the Python Standard Library documentation for the pinned
CPython release, under their license, and marked which facilities were Core
and which belonged to hosts. Revision 2 replaces it with an original text:

1. **Structure.** The document is organized as ECMA-262 is: conformance and
   notation; data types and abstract operations; execution contexts and object
   behaviours; source text and grammar; expressions, statements, functions,
   classes, and modules; then the built-in namespace and the intrinsic objects
   by kind. The grammar summary, host hooks, library facades, and
   implementation-defined items are annexes.
2. **Algorithms.** Semantics that revision 1 gave in prose are given as
   numbered algorithms with the `?` and `!` completion shorthands of 5.2, so
   that the order of evaluation, of lookups, and of checks is stated.
3. **No library dependence.** Revision 1 specified objects through the modules
   that expose them (`types`, `typing`, `annotationlib`, `string.templatelib`,
   `sys`, `abc`, `copyreg`, `codecs`, `warnings`, `site`). Revision 2 defines
   those objects as intrinsics reachable from the language and the facilities
   behind the modules as host hooks (Annex B); the modules are described only
   informatively (Annex C).
4. **Normative messages.** Exception types and message texts are normative in
   the Core profile (5.2.6), including the dotted type names of intrinsics
   outside `builtins`.
5. **Codecs.** Only UTF-8, ASCII, and Latin-1 and the seven error handlers of
   23.3 are Core; other encodings are host-supplied.
6. **Decisions recorded.** Questions revision 1 left to the reference's prose
   are decided: the identity of empty immutable containers and of `copy()`
   results is implementation-defined (Annex D); a match that fails after
   binding names may leave them bound (Annex D); hash algorithms for numbers,
   tuples, and frozensets are specified (22.1.9, 25.2, 26.3.4); the operand
   order of reflected operators and comparisons is specified (7.2, 7.6); class
   creation stores `__firstlineno__` and `__static_attributes__` (16.4.2).
7. **Removed material.** The glossary, the history and license chapters, the
   upstream license notice, and the source integrity manifest of revision 1
   are not part of revision 2, which contains no upstream text.

- The Python Enhancement Proposals that introduced the features specified
